ab 10 November 2026
Global Research
All Ring Tech
Bright 2027 outlook with multiple drivers
Intact 2027 outlook
All Ring's 2027 outlook is robust, supported by three key drivers. 1) We expect TSMC
and ASE to continue accelerating CoWoS capacity expansion, with industry capacity
reaching ~270kwpm by end-2027E, up from ~160kwpm by end-2026. We estimate
TSMC and ASE’s capacity could reach ~180kwpm and ~70kwpm, respectively, by
2027E. 2) Second, with Intel's EMIB-T progressing on track toward volume production

by early 2028, we expect related equipment demand to begin ramping in 2027,
providing a new growth driver for All Ring. 3) CPO’s fiber array unit (FAU) coupling tools
start contributing to All Ring's 2027 revenue, with demand from Nvidia and AMD.
More concrete CPO development in 2027-28
Our industry feedback suggests Nvidia is taking the lead to aggressively push CPO mass
production in 2027 for scale up switch solution. AMD is also engaging with key

equipment suppliers, in preparation for competing CPO solution. Competition among
the equipment vendors is fierce, but we believe All Ring is well positioned in FAU
coupling as a tier 1 supplier, given the superior execution in driving higher tool
throughput versus peers. Considering the meaningful tool demand in 2027-28, we
forecast All Ring to recognize ~100 tools for revenue in 2027E, contributing ~12% of All
Ring's sales next year. Potential upside to our current estimates could come from larger

TAM for faster capacity expansion, All Ring securing higher share / ASP through better
tool performance.
Lower Q3 gross margin due to mix
Q3 EPS came in below LSEG consensus by -10% as gross margin of 48.5% missed
consensus of 54.2%. Lower gross margin could be due to a higher mix of automation
products, which carries a lower margin vs corporate average. For Q4, considering lighter
seasonality and current visibility, we model sales to decline by -28% from Q3 with gross

margin of 53% and EPS of NT$7.57.
Valuation: Maintain Buy and target of $1700
Post Q3 results, we fine-tune 2026 EPS by -4% due to lower gross margin in the quarter,
though our estimates for 2027/28 are largely kept unchanged. Our 2027E/2028E EPS
are 3%/30% ahead of Consensus pre-earnings. We maintain our Buy rating and price
target of $1700 based on 27x average 2027-28E PE.

Forecast returns

Forecast excess return 61.5%
Company Description
Founded in 1996, All Ring is a Taiwan-based supplier of automation equipment for the
semiconductor back-end packaging and passive component manufacturing industries. The
company's products include underfill dispensers, heatsink placement and automated optical
inspection (AOI) tools for advanced packaging equipment, particularly for heterogeneous

integration. Revenue is generated primarily from sales of semiconductor and electronic
manufacturing equipment and related automation solutions, with more than 90% of
revenue derived from Taiwan.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value All Ring based on PE methodology.
Risks for All Ring and the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in

the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less
investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech

companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in All Ring
gaining/losing equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs.
- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in All
Ring losing market share from major foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could

lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to All
Ring, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.
- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future equipment orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact All Ring’s
margins for equipment shipments.

Quantitative Research Review
UBS Global Research publishes a quantitative assessment of its analysts' responses to certain questions about the likelihood of an occurrence
of a number of short term factors in a product known as the 'Quantitative Research Review'.  The views for this month can be found below.
Views contained in this assessment on a particular stock reflect only the views on those short term factors which are a different timeframe to

the 12-month timeframe reflected in any equity rating set out in this note.  For previous responses please make reference to (i) previous UBS
Global Research reports; and (ii) where no applicable research report was published that month, the Quantitative Research Review which can
be found at https://neo.ubs.com/quantitative, or contact your UBS sales representative for access to the report or the Quantitative Research
Team on ubs-quant-answers@ubs.com. A consolidated report which contains all responses is also available and again you should contact your

UBS sales representative for details and pricing or the Quantitative Research Team on the email above.
All Ring Tech
Question Response
1. Is the industry structure facing the firm likely to improve or deteriorate over the
next six months? Rate on a scale of 1-5 (1 = getting worse, 3 = no change, 5 =
getting better, N/A = no view)

2. Is the regulatory/government environment facing the firm likely to improve or
deteriorate over the next six months? Rate on a scale of 1-5 (1 = getting tougher

by the analyst to indicate that the stock's price target and/or rating may change in the near term, usually in response to an event that

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.

Update
Co-Tech Development Corp.  | Asia Pacific
Key Japanese Competitor
Announced Price Hikes
What's new? Our supply chain checks suggest that one of Co-Tech's key Japanese
competitors notified customers earlier this week of price increases for its
electrodeposited (ED) copper foil products. The company will raise prices by 30%
for ¼ oz (9µm) and ⅓ oz (12µm) ED copper foils and by 15% for other thicknesses,
effective for shipments from December 2026 onward. This aligns with our previous

supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report (link).
Our view: The increases extend beyond high-end HVLP products to copper foils
used in consumer electronics. We believe this reflects rising production costs and,
more importantly, tightening supply, with positive implications for Co-Tech. We
expect Co-Tech to follow with further price increases, although likely at smaller
magnitudes given the ~10% hike it implemented in October.
Morgan Stanley Taiwan Limited+

Rachel L Zhang
Equity Analyst

Key Takeaways
Earlier this week, a key Japanese competitor issued a price increase notice to its
customers for electrodeposited copper foils.
This aligns with our  supply chain check findings published in our Nov 4 report.
We believe the  price hikes have positive implications for Co-Tech and expect Co-
Tech to follow with price increases as well.

However, since Co-Tech already implemented a ~10% price hike in October, we
don't expect it to announce a price hike of the same magnitude.

Analyst Stock Ratings
Overweight (O). The stock's total return is expected to exceed the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next
12-18 months.
Equal-weight (E). The stock's total return is expected to be in line with the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over

the next 12-18 months.
Not-Rated (NR). Currently the analyst does not have adequate conviction about the stock's total return relative to the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage
universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next 12-18 months.
Underweight (U). The stock's total return is expected to be below the average total return of the analyst's industry (or industry team's) coverage universe, on a risk-adjusted basis, over the next

12-18 months.

江誼蓁 
yizhen0808@yuanta.com

3Q26 EPS 低於市場預期 14%，預期 4Q26 毛利率小幅回升 
嘉澤 3Q26 營收 99.58 億元，季增 4.1%/年增 18.2%，其中伺服器營收年增
55%，惟記憶體與 PCB 缺料壓抑 CPU Socket 拉貨，原物料成本維持高檔，
加上新平台導入慢於預期，毛利率季減 0.6 個百分點至 44.8%。薪資費用高
於預期以及業外淨收益亦較上季下降，EPS 為 17.06 元，低於本中心/市場
預期 11.0%/14.4%。考量缺料延續及新平台挹注有限，下修 4Q26 營收
5.8%至 103.89 億元，季增 4.3%/年增 12.2%，毛利率估季增 0.3 個百分點
至 45.1%，EPS 下修 12.6%至 18.79 元。 
QD 擴產及伺服器平台換代帶動 2027 年營收年增 30% 
QD 產能滿載，公司目標於未來一季將產能倍增，預估 QD 占總營收比重於
2026 年 12 月達 3–5%，2027 年全年接近 10%。AMD Venice 預計自 1Q27
末加速放量，2027 年平均採用率約 20%。Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27
末至 2Q27 初導入，2H27 採用率估達 10–15%。新平台有助提升 CPU 
Socket 單價及 PCIe 6.0 連接器採用率，預估 2027 年伺服器營收年增 50%，
帶動整體營收年增 30%。 
維持買進及目標價 2,200 元 
伺服器缺料及 PC 業務疲弱使營收低於前估，原物料成本居高、新平台滲透
慢於預期，加上低於公司平均毛利率的 QD 占比提高及薪資費用增加，下修
2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%至 74.92/106.29 元。評價方面，本中心仍看
好 2027 年 QD 擴產將擴大營收與獲利貢獻，Venice 與 Oak Stream 逐步放
量則有助改善 CPU Socket 產品組合。隨營收規模擴大，預估 2027 年營益
率由 24.5%回升至 28.3%，帶動 EPS 年增 41.9%，因此維持買進評等，以 
2027 年預估 EPS 106.29 元 21 倍本益比推得目標價 2,200 元。 
元大觀點 
⧫  
3Q26 EPS 為 17.06 元，低於市場預期 14%，主因薪資費用提升以及
原物料成本居高、新平台導入進度不如預期，毛利率季減至 44.8%。 
⧫  
記憶體與 PCB 缺料持續壓抑 CPU Socket 拉貨，下修 4Q26 EPS 
12.6%至 18.79 元，預期毛利率小幅回升。 
⧫  
QD 放量與 AMD/Intel 新平台導入帶動 2027 年營收年增 30%、EPS
年增 42%至 106.29 元，維持買進及目標價 2,200 元。

### Page 2

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第2 頁，共 16頁
營運分析
3Q26 毛利率低於預期，薪資費用提升，EPS 低於市場預期 14%
嘉澤 3Q26 營收 99.58 億元，季增 4.1%/年增 18.2%，低於本中心/市場預期 1.7%/2.5%，其中伺服
器營收年增 55%，占總營收 53.1%，惟記憶體與 PCB 缺料持續限制客戶出貨，CPU Socket 拉貨
低於預期。原物料成本維持高檔，伺服器新平台導入慢於預期，加上部分桌機客戶因缺料改採支援
DDR4 或 DDR3 的舊平台，使低單價、低毛利產品占比偏高，毛利率季減 0.6 個百分點至 44.8%，
低於本中心/市場預期 0.7/0.9 個百分點。中國廠區自 2Q26 起調薪，使費用率年增約 0.7 個百分點。
受惠於營收規模擴大，費用率仍季減 1.6 個百分點至 21.9%，營益率升至 22.9%，營業利益季增
8.6%至 22.84 億元。業外淨收益由上季 4.95 億元降至 0.95 億元，歸屬母公司淨利季減 3.3%至

19.19 億元。EPS 為 17.06 元，季減 3.5%/年減 17.6%，低於本中心/市場預期 11.0%/14.4%。

新平台導入速度不如預期，下修 4Q26 EPS 13%至 18.79 元
嘉澤 10 月營收 32.0 億元，月減 5%/年增 12%，達本中心 4Q26 營收原估的 29%。公司預期 4Q26
營收呈低個位數季增，零組件缺料仍是主要出貨限制，因此本中心下修 4Q26 營收 5.8%至 103.89
億元，季增 4.3%/年增 12.2%。SOCAMM 等新品出貨增加有助毛利率回升，惟 AMD Venice 年底
採用率僅估 3–5%，Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27 末至 2Q27 初開始導入，高針腳數 CPU Socket

對 4Q26 產品組合的挹注低於前估，加上原物料成本維持高檔，以及毛利率低於公司平均的 QD 出
貨增加，本中心下修 4Q26 毛利率 1.4 個百分點至 45.1%，季增 0.3 個百分點。預估營業利益季增
10.2%至 25.17 億元，EPS 下修 12.6%至 18.79 元，季增約 10%/年減 16.5%。

QD 擴產與伺服器平台換代帶動 2027 年營收年增 30%
本中心預估嘉澤 2027 年營收年增 30%，主要成長動能來自 QD 放量與通用伺服器平台換代。公司
預估 2027 年伺服器整機出貨量年增 10–15%，新平台導入也將推升 CPU Socket 單價，並帶動
PCIe 6.0 連接器採用率提升。AMD Venice 目前採用率不到 3%，預計自 1Q27 末加速放量，2027
年全年採用率約 20%。Intel Oak Stream 預計於 1Q27 末至 2Q27 初開始導入，2H27 採用率估達

10–15%，營收貢獻主要落在下半年。隨 PCIe 連接器與線材市占率提升，本中心預估 2026/2027
年伺服器營收分別年增 56%/50%。嘉澤另預計於 2H27 開始出貨 Arm CPU 平台的客製化 Socket。
QD 目前產能滿載，公司目標於未來一季將產能倍增。預估 QD 占總營收比重於 2026 年 12 月達
3–5%，2027 年全年接近 10%。QD 放量將稀釋整體毛利率，但新增營業費用相對有限，規模擴大
後仍可挹注營業利益及 EPS。隨伺服器新平台採用率提高及 SOCAMM 出貨增加，本中心預估
2027 年毛利率由 46.2%回升至 48.5%。NPO Socket 預計於 2027 年以總頻寬 3.2Tbps 的產品小量
出貨，占營收不到 2%，2028 年隨單價較高的 6.4Tbps 產品導入，營收貢獻將更為明顯。
非伺服器業務方面，公司預估 2027 年 PC 市場出貨量再減約 10%。本中心估嘉澤 2026/2027 年筆
電營收分別年減 17%/4%，桌機營收年減 30%/年增 1%;策略客戶營收年增 3%/2%，車用年增

27%/15%，工業及醫療年增 22%/20%。嘉基受惠於高速線材出貨成長，營收預估年增 12%/40%。

產業概況  
2026 年 PC 出貨恐衰退近雙位數 
記憶體價格大漲，影響 PC 出貨。受到上游記憶體成本大幅上揚，PC 廠商即使轉嫁或降低規格，
均將影響消費者購買意願，因此預估 2026 年 PC(NB+DT)出貨衰退 9.6%，NB 出貨亦年減 9.7%。
2027 年 PC 及 NB 出貨量才有機會回升低個位數。 
上半年消費者提前拉貨，下半年出貨待觀察。1H26 市場預期記憶體及儲存裝置未來大漲，因此客
戶有提前拉貨現象，IDC 統計全球 PC 出貨 679 萬台，季減 16%，但年增 3%，2Q26 起 PC 步入
衰退 5%，下半年更衰退至 15~20%。

### Page 6

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第6 頁，共 16頁  
AI、ASIC、通用型強勢出擊，2026/2027 年美系 CSP 資本支出估年增 95%/46%  
在 ChatGPT 等 AI 應用陸續推向市場，各大業者紛紛開始研發自有超級大模型，包括 Gemini 
(Google)、Claude (Anthropic)、Llama (Meta) 等，各大品牌開始大舉投入大型語言模型(LLM)市場，
且每一個產品的智慧皆持續提高，部分消費者也陸續從 ChatGPT 陣營轉移至 Gemini 陣營，反映
出消費者開始逐漸提升對 AI 的黏著度。根據 Grand View Research 調查，2024 年全球 AI 市場營
收約 2,792 億美元，預估 2030 年市場規模超過 3 兆美元，擁有超過 30% 的年複合增長率。 
面對這爆發性的長期成長趨勢，CSP 業者(Amazon、Microsoft、Google、Meta)在法說會上一致強
調，AI 投資已轉變為一項橫跨 2026 年的結構性戰略，CSP 大廠不僅持續擴大對 GB300 等次世代
AI 伺服器的採購，更將重點放在資料中心基礎設施的長期戰略規劃，以應對 AI 運算對電力及散熱
系統強勁成長的需求，確保未來數年內擁有足夠的能源與容量來支持持續迭代的大模型部署。本中
心認為，2026 年美國主要 CSP 資本支出將年增 95%，且將有一半的成長來自於資料中心建置，代
表了資料中心的不足及未來伺服器需求。明年不僅僅是 NVIDIA 的天下，AWS、Google、Meta 
ASIC 的崛起也將是一大關鍵，其中又以 Google TPU 需求最為強勁，已經有兩個客戶預計將採購
TPU。 
另外，受惠於 AI 訓練與推論需求爆發的連帶效應，2026/2027 年通用型伺服器預估出貨量將年增
10%/9%，其中以存儲型伺服器需求最為強勁。隨著生成式 AI 應用普及和使用者數量暴增，CSP
等業者必須不斷擴充容量，且不僅限於高階 GPU 伺服器，龐大的 AI 模型訓練和推論在使用狀態中
將消耗大量的數據(DATA)，使得存儲型伺服器的需求大增。

### Page 7

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第7 頁，共 16頁  
ASIC 進入高速成長期，GPU 需求仍維持擴張 
從 AI 晶片預期出貨來看，GPU 與 ASIC 需求均持續成長，但 2026 年後 ASIC 成長動能明顯加速。
Nvidia GPU 出貨量預估由 2025 年 565.7 萬顆增至 2026 年 650 萬顆、2027 年 850 萬顆，年增
15%及 31%；AMD 亦由 2025 年 90 萬顆增至 2027 年 180 萬顆，顯示通用 GPU 算力需求仍維持
成長。 
相較之下，ASIC 受惠 CSP 加速自研晶片及導入規模擴大，總出貨量預估由 2025 年 380 萬顆大幅
增至 2026 年 725 萬顆、2027 年 1,360 萬顆，年增率達 91%及 88%，成長速度明顯高於 GPU。其
中 Google 仍為最大 ASIC 使用者，2027 年用量預估達 800 萬顆，AWS 亦增至 320 萬顆，
Microsoft 及 Meta 同步擴大部署。我們認為，在資料中心電力逐漸成為算力擴張瓶頸下，CSP 將
更重視每瓦運算效能及整體運算成本，ASIC 針對特定運算工作的效率優勢有望推升滲透率，惟
GPU 憑藉通用性及軟體生態系優勢，仍將維持重要地位。

請參閱末頁之免責宣言。 台灣：電子零組件 2026 年 11 月 16 日 Lotes 第8 頁，共 16頁
獲利調整與股票評價
下修 2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%，維持買進及目標價 2,200 元
伺 服 器 供 應 鏈 缺 料 持 續 壓 抑 CPU Socket 拉 貨 ， 筆 電 及 桌 機 營 收 亦 低 於 前 估 ， 本 中 心 下 修
2026/2027 年營收 2.0%/2.6%至 392.43/510.58 億元，分別年增 16.2%/30.1%。原物料成本維持高
檔，AMD 及 Intel 新平台採用率提升慢於預期，加上 QD 營收占比提高，使毛利率改善幅度低於前
估。本中心下修 2026/2027 年毛利率 0.5/0.6 個百分點至 46.2%/48.5%。費用方面，中國廠區調薪
推升固定薪資支出，公司預估 2027 年單季營業費用約 22–25 億元。考量薪資費用高於前估，下修
2026/2027 年 EPS 6.0%/5.4%至 74.92/106.29 元，分別年增 6.8%/41.9%。

評價方面，本中心仍看好 2027 年 QD 擴產將擴大營收與獲利貢獻，Venice 與 Oak Stream 逐步放
量則有助改善 CPU Socket 產品組合。隨營收規模擴大，預估 2027 年營益率由 24.5%回升至
28.3%，帶動 EPS 年增 41.9%，因此維持買進評等，以 2027 年預估 EPS 106.29 元 21 倍本益比
推得目標價 2,200 元。

公司簡介  
嘉澤成立於 1986 年，專門生產及設計 PCIe、DDR、SATA 等連接器及 USB 連接埠，公司擁有完整的生產流程，包含設計、
加工、成型、沖壓、電鍍及自動化組裝，可提供客製化需求及標準型產品。公司為全球前三大 Server Socket (CPU 插槽)供
應商，市佔率約為 30-35%，同業為鴻海集團(鴻騰精密)及安費諾等。該產業的進入門檻高，多數產品皆已申請專利，且公
司實驗室已通過 ISO 認證，可縮短測試時間，並降低測試費用。嘉澤產品為電腦、伺服器、消費性電子產品之各式連接器
及零組件，旗下有四大事業體，分別是 PC/NB、伺服器、子公司嘉基及其他業務(包括 Type-C 連接器)。

ESG 分析  
分別藉由「在 ESG 議題上的曝險」和「個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力」兩個面向來衡量公司於 ESG 上的表現。 
► ESG 總分： 嘉澤整體的 ESG 風險評級屬於低風險，於 Sustainalytics 資料庫所覆蓋的公司中排名亦排行於中間偏前的
位置，在電子零組件行業中的公司中排名略領先於同業。 
► 在 ESG 議題上的曝險： 嘉澤的整體曝險屬於中等水準，優於電子零組件行業的平均水準。公司較需要改善的 ESG 議
題包含公司治理、商業道德與資源使用等。 
► 個別公司在 ESG 議題上的執行力：嘉澤在針對重大 ESG 議題的管理水準和執行力屬中等。公司遵循適當的 ESG 揭露，
表明對投資者和公眾的強烈責任感。其 ESG 相關問題由董事會或經營團隊負責，建議將這些整合到核心業務戰略中。

主要負責撰寫本研究報告全文或部分內容之分析師，茲針對本報告所載證券或證券發行機構，於此聲明：(1) 文中所述觀點皆準確反映其個人對各證券或

投資評等說明 
買進：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持正面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中增持該股。 
持有-超越同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力高於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
持有-落後同業：本中心認為根據目前股價，該檔個股基本面吸引力低於同業。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股發展前景、財務表現、利多題材、評價資訊
以及風險概況之分析。 
賣出：根據本中心對該檔個股投資期間絕對或相對報酬率之預測，我們對該股持負面觀點。此一觀點係基於本中心對該股之發展前景、財務表現、利多題
材、評價資訊以及風險概況之分析。建議投資人於投資部位中減持該股。 
評估中：本中心之預估、評等、目標價尚在評估中，但仍積極追蹤該個股。 
限制評等：為遵循相關法令規章及/或元大之政策，暫不給予評等及目標價。

註：元大給予個股之目標價係依 12 個月投資期間計算。大中華探索系列報告並無正式之 12 個月目標價，其投資建議乃根據分析師報告中之指定期間分析
而得。

주가 추이

3Q 큰 폭의 판매 감소, 매출/영업이익 시장 기대치 하회 예상 
3Q26 동사 매출액과 영업이익은 각각 44.6조원(-4.5% 이하 YoY)과 2.4조원
(-5.4%) 기록하며 시장 기대치 하회 전망. 3Q 매출액은 글로벌 판매 부진 지속
과 환율 하락, 그리고 친환경차 판매 성장 둔화 등의 영향에 기인. 특히, 3Q 판
매량은 918.7만대(-11.5% 이하 YoY)로 전년 동기 대비 큰 폭 감소하며 매출
액에 부정적 영향. 이는 7월과 8월 발생한 파업 영향으로 약 5.5만대 규모의 생
산 물량 차질이 발생한 부분에 기인. 파업은 기존 물량/믹스 개선에 기여했던 
글로벌 HEV 판매도 둔화(+3.4%)시키면서 매출에 부정적 영향을 심화. 
3Q 수익성은 4~5월 상승했던 원자재 투입에 따른 원가 상승 영향과 친환경차 
판매 감소에 따른 부정적 믹스 영향 불가피. 이에 시장 기대치인 3.0조원을 크
게 하회할 전망. 다만, 기말 환율이 1,356원/$ 수준으로 낮아지면서 판매보증비 
외화환산손익 영향은 다소 개선(0.4%p) 예상되며, 관세 영향 또한 전년 동기 
대비 약 1조원(2.1%p) 감소하면서 수익성 악화 영향을 일부 만회할 전망.  
’27년 지연된 신차효과 가시화로 반등 여력 충분 
4Q부터는 3Q 파업 영향 등으로 지연된 신차 확대 본격화되고, 현지 수요 높은 
차종 중심의 물량 확대를 통해 매출 회복 및 이익 성장 가시화가 기대. 아반떼
와 투싼 FMC 확대로 국내 및 해외 판매 둔화가 점진적 만회되고, 지난 9월부
터 터키에서 아이오닉3 생산이 본격화되면서 유럽에서의 시장 부진 회복이 기
대. 또한, GV80과 G80 HEV 모델 출시로 내년부터 국내/북미 중심 판매 확대
되며 올해 부진했던 물량/믹스 개선에 기여할 것으로 기대. ’27년 HMGMA에
서 HEV 신차 생산 투입 예정되어 있어 상대적으로 높은 수요가 지속되는 북미
에서의 HEV 현지 생산 확대로 가동률 증가와 관세 부담 완화도 수익성 개선에 
기여할 것으로 기대. 
부정적 환율 고려 필요하나 ’27년 강한 증익 기대  
’27년 동사 영업이익 추정치는 12.4조원(+22.3%)으로 ’26년 감익에서 ’27년 
증익 전환 예상. 최근 환율 추세 고려 시 내년 상반기까지 부정적인 환율 영향 
확대 가능성 존재하나, 기존 노후 차종에서 현지 수요 대응 가능한 신차 중심 
생산/판매 확대로 물량/믹스 개선되며 부정적 환율 영향 상쇄 기대.  
12M EPS 추정치 하향(-11.8%)과 상반기 적용한 코스피 프리미엄 제거(-
10%)에 따라 목표주가를 670,000원으로 하향. 그러나, 연 내 RMAC 운영 
성과와 RA 설립, 12월 모셔널 자율주행 상용화와 웨이모向 로보택시 
양산/공급 등 미래 사업에서의 가시성도 여전히 기대 가능한 부분.

[ Compliance Notice ] (공표일: 2026 년 10 월 8 일) 
이 자료는 조사분석 담당자가 객관적 사실에 근거해 작성하였으며, 타인의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 본인의 견을 정확하게 
반영했습니다. 본인은 이 자료에서 다룬 종목과 관련해 공표일 현재 관련 법규상 알려야 할 재산적 이해관계가 없습니다. 본인은 이 
자료를 기관투자자 또는 제 3 자에게 사전에 제공한 사실이 없습니다. (김성래) 
저희 회사는 공표일 현재 이 자료에서 다룬 종목의 발행주식을 1% 이상 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
이 자료는 투자자의 증권투자를 돕기 위해 당사 고객에 한하여 배포되는 자료로서 저작권이 당사에 있으며 불법 복제 및 배포를 금
합니다. 이 자료에 수록된 내용은 당사 리서치센터가 신뢰할 만한 자료나 정보출처로부터 얻은 것이지만, 당사는 그 정확성이나 완
전성을 보장할 수 없습니다. 따라서 이 자료는 어떠한 경우에도 고객의 증권투자 결과와 관련된 법적 책임소재에 대한 증빙으로 사
용될 수 없습니다.

### Page 8

현대차 (005380) [한화리서치]

8 
[ 종목 투자등급 ] 
당사는 개별 종목에 대해 향후 1 년간 +15% 이상의 절대수익률이 기대되는 종목에 대해 Buy(매수) 의견을 제시합니다. 또한 절대수
익률 -15~+15%가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Hold(보유) 의견을, -15% 이하가 예상되는 종목에 대해 Sell(매도) 의견을 제시합니다. 밸류
에이션 방법 등 절대수익률 산정은 개별 종목을 커버하는 애널리스트의 추정에 따르며, 목표주가 산정이나 투자의견 변경 주기는 종
목별로 다릅니다.

[ 산업 투자의견 ] 
당사는 산업에 대해 향후 1 년간 해당 업종의 수익률이 과거 수익률에 비해 양호한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Positive(긍정
적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 또한 향후 1 년간 수익률이 과거 수익률과 유사한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Neutral(중립적) 
의견을, 과거 수익률보다 부진한 흐름을 보일 것으로 예상되는 경우에 Negative(부정적) 의견을 제시하고 있습니다. 산업별 수익률 전
망은 해당 산업 내 분석대상 종목들에 대한 담당 애널리스트의 분석과 판단에 따릅니다.

3Q26 Review: “조삼모사” Again  
3Q26P 연결 실적은 매출액 6조 943억원(YoY -6.0%), 영업이익 4,520억원(YoY -46.2%, OPM 
7.4%)을 기록하며 컨센서스 대비 연결 매출액(7.5조원)과 영업이익(1.16조원) 모두 대폭 하회했다. 주
요 자회사가 양호한 실적을 기록했음에도 불구하고, 지상방산 실적이 매우 부진했다. 지상방산은 매출
액 1조 2,455억원(YoY -41.0%), 영업이익 217억원(YoY -96.2%, OPM 1.7%)을 기록했는데, 1)3Q 
생산 물량이 4Q 매출 인식되는 흐름 속, 고정비 부담이 컸었고, 2)낮은 수출 비중(3Q 48%, 2Q 58%)
과 국내외 사업의 부진한 믹스(국내 BEP 수준, 해외 OPM 11% 추정), 3)비우호적인 원/달러 환율, 4)
대전 사업장 사고 복구 및 기타 일회성 비용(300억원+160억원, 총 460억원) 등이 반영되었다.   
대전 사업장 정상화 시점과 사업 차질 영향은? 
대전 사업장은 내년 상반기 중 정상화될 예정이며, 보은과 여수 공장에서 생산 보완을 위한 조치가 진
행 중인 것으로 소통했다. 또한 대전 사업장은 국내 물량 위주이며, 수출 납기(천무, 천궁-II 추진체 
등)에는 지장이 없을 것이라고 언급했지만, 기타 사업장 램프업을 위한 인건비 증가 등, 수익성 악화는 
불가피할 것으로 예상된다.  
15%대의 매출 성장 가이던스는 달성이 가능할까? 
보수적으로 접근하는 것이 합리적이라는 판단이다. 당사는 ‘26E 지상방산 매출액을 일부 ‘27~28E로 
이연하면서 -6.1% 하향 조정했다. 사측은 폴란드 EC1 천무 인도 가이던스를 기존 40문에서 60문으
로 상향 조정하는 등 납품 속도가 예정대비 빨라진 것을 언급했다. 다만, 천무 EC1에서 미사일 물량 
비중이 높은 것으로 파악되기 때문에 생산 지연 가능성과 수출입 데이터 상 12월 출하 물량의 매출 인
식 이월 가능성을 반영했다. ‘27E는 폴란드 후속 사업과 루마니아 K9, 호주 레드백 등이 양산 페이즈
에 진입하며 성장세가 지속될 전망이다.    
투자의견 Buy, 목표주가 1,480,000원 유지 
실적 쇼크와는 별개로, 미국 K9MH 시제, 스페인 K9, 크로아티아 천무 등 수주는 서프라이즈라고 판
단된다. 분기말 수주잔고는 35조원(수출 비중 74%, 스페인 미포함)으로 중장기 성장성에 대한 이견은 
없다. 불가피한 분기 실적 변동으로 동사의 미래 가치를 예단하는 것은 시기상조라는 판단이다.

## Document 306: Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Capital Market Outlook (2026-10-13).pdf

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook

### Page 2

2 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MACRO STRATEGY
Consumers Feel One Way, but Spend Another
Kirsten Cabacungan, Vice President and Senior Investment Strategist
Americans have spent much of the past several years telling surveys they are cautious
about the economy. Yet they continue to spend as though they are not. The University
of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index remains near multidecade lows, while the
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence has also been in a yearslong downtrend.

These surveys reflect a list of persistent concerns from elevated food and gas prices to
geopolitical uncertainty and labor market anxiety.
Even so, consumer spending has remained remarkably resilient. The final estimate of Q2
real consumer spending, as measured by PCE, was revised up to an annualized rate of
3.8%, well above the 2.3% 20-year average. More recent data suggest that momentum
has continued. According to Bank of America Institute, the latest monthly total credit and

debit card spending data per household rose 6.2% year-over-year (YoY) in September, and
spending excluding gasoline was up 5.1% YoY, both the third strongest growth rates in the
past four years.1
The persistence of consumer spending is particularly notable given that temporary cyclical
boosts like larger tax refunds are starting to fade. That raises an important question: What is
actually funding strong consumption today? Recent data suggest the drivers are becoming

increasingly more diversified, with broadening labor market trends, healthy consumer
finances and elevated wealth effects all reinforcing one another and underpinning consumers.
First, the labor market continues to provide an important foundation for consumer spending,
and recent trends suggest wage growth is supporting a broader set of households than in
recent years. Jobs growth has slowed from the exceptionally strong pace seen following the
pandemic, but broader indicators suggest labor market conditions remain relatively stable.

Although the September jobs report came in softer than expected, the three- and six-month
rolling averages indicate that the trend in jobs growth likely bottomed out last year and has
gradually improved since (Exhibit 1A). Meanwhile, the unemployment rate remains low by
historical standards and layoffs remain contained. And while wage growth has moderated
and recently trailed headline inflation (Exhibit 1B), cumulative income gains over the past
several years have likely helped many households absorb higher prices better.

An important development though has been improving wage growth trends among lower-
income households, where spending tends to be most sensitive to changes in wages. Bank
of America Institute consumer deposit account data show after-tax wage growth for lower-
income households outpaced higher-income households for a second consecutive month
in August, rising 4.7% YoY compared with 3.5% for higher-income households. Importantly,
this improvement appears to be supporting discretionary spending, not just spending

on necessities. Lower-income discretionary spending rose 5.7% YoY in August, nearly
matching the 5.9% pace for higher-income households. In other words, the narrowing gap
in wage and spending growth across income cohorts suggests consumer resilience looks
to be becoming less dependent on affluent households alone, creating a broader and more
durable foundation for consumption.
Second, household finances remain in relatively good shape, providing consumers with the

flexibility to continue spending. The ratio of household debt to disposable personal income
remains near its lowest level since the late 1990s, excluding the temporary distortions
caused by pandemic-related income support in 2020 and 2021 (Exhibit 1C). Recent
revisions to the Bureau of Economic Analysis national income accounts also suggest
consumers have a larger financial buffer than previous data suggested. Personal saving
estimates over the past year have been revised higher by $320 billion, or 39%, and the

revised savings rate remains above 4% after previous estimates showed it fell to 3% during
the summer.2 Concerns that consumers may be rapidly exhausting their savings buffers
may therefore be overstated. Supporting this view, Bank of America Institute consumer
1 Bank of America Institute, “Consumer Checkpoint: Strength in numbers.” As of October 9, 2026.
2 Bank of America, “Massive Savings Markup = Another Reason to Be Bullish.” As of October 2, 2026.

Investment Implications
Consumer spending strength
remains a key pillar of economic
and earnings growth, which keeps
us constructive on our Equity
overweight. The growing connection
between household wealth and
spending reinforces the importance
of maintaining a diversified portfolio.

### Page 3

3 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
deposit account data show that middle- and lower-income households continue to maintain
deposit balances above prepandemic levels across age groups.
Third, elevated household wealth continues to provide a powerful tailwind for consumer
spending, particularly among households that account for an outsized share of overall
consumption. U.S. household net worth rose $12.8 trillion (T) to a record $195.9T in Q2,

driven largely by gains in Equities, which contributed $10.7T of the increase (Exhibit 1D).
As a result, household net worth relative to disposable income climbed to a record 8.3,
meaning households now hold more than eight dollars of wealth for every dollar of annual
disposable income. Households are also sitting on $35.8T in home equity, an all-time high
level that provides a significant balance sheet cushion. Rising asset values have helped to

support spending through what economists refer to as wealth effects. In other words, when
households feel wealthier, they tend to be more willing to spend.
For investors, the key takeaway is that consumer spending is being supported by multiple
engines despite the weaker sentiment data. Labor income trends are supporting wider
participation, healthy balance sheets are helping households absorb economic pressures,
and elevated household wealth continues to sustain higher-income spending. Together,

these forces are supportive of economic growth and corporate profits, which is particularly
important since the consumer accounts for roughly 70% of U.S. gross domestic product.
That said, it does not eliminate risk. A meaningful risk to the consumer would be a scenario
in which both the labor and wealth engines weaken simultaneously. For now, labor market
conditions remain stable, and wealth effects remain supportive. Equity market pullbacks in
recent years have done little to derail consumer spending, suggesting it would likely take a

deeper and more prolonged drawdown to alter consumer behavior.
Exhibit 1: Consumer Spending is Being Supported by Multiple Engines.

4 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
MARKET VIEW
The Bull Market Turns Four: Older, Unloved—But Still Kicking
Joseph Quinlan, Managing Director and Head of Market Strategy
Ariana Chiu, Assistant Vice President and Investment Strategist
Four years ago this month, virtually no one was in the mood to buy U.S. Equities, and for
good reasons.
Rewind to October 2022: Inflation the month before clocked in at an annual rate of 8.2%.

The yield curve was inverted, a signal that has preceded most past recessions. Consumer
sentiment was abysmal, having dropped to a record low in June 2022. Recession
concerns were mounting, with an October Wall Street Journal poll survey putting the
odds of a recession within 12 months at about 63%. And realizing (belatedly) that
inflation was not “transitory,” the Fed was in the midst of administering one of the most
aggressive monetary tightening cycles in decades, raising the fed funds rate five times

between March and September 2022 by a stunning 300 basis points.
Reflecting the mood at the moment, the S&P 500 closed at 3,577 on October 12, 2022,
down 25% below its January peak, leaving investors battered and bruised but blind to the
fact that the market had bottomed. Four years on, the bull market that emerged from
the gloom of 2022 is still standing and kicking, with the S&P 500 up roughly 130% since
the October 2022 lows.
The more than doubling of the S&P over the past four years has hardly been linear—and

has been accompanied with plenty of pain and doubt. Exhibit 2 maps the rocky path
trodden by the bull over the past four years. Less than six months into the rally, the
markets were rocked by the failure of Silicon Valley Bank (SVB) on March 10, 2023. The
S&P Regional Banks Select Industry Index lost 18% that week and stoked concerns of
massive deposit runs and credit stress across the capital markets. However, owing to
emergency intervention by the government, lower yields and strength elsewhere in the

markets, the crisis passed. Some banks failed that month, but the bull market didn’t.
Exhibit 2: Revisiting the Rocky Path Trodden by the Bull.
Period "Reason to Sell" What happened instead
October 2022 Inflation >8%, aggressive Fed tightening, recession concerns Bull market begins
March 2023 SVB/regional banking crisis Stocks keep climbing
2023-24 Inverted yield curve, “recession inevitable” Economy keeps expanding
2024 “Magnificent 7* bubble,” narrow breadth, expensive valuations Earnings propel market higher

2025 Tariffs, trade war, policy uncertainty Market absorbs another shock
2026 Oil/diesel shock, 5%+ yields, Fed tightening, geopolitical risks Bull reaches fourth birthday
*Apple, Amazon, Alphabet, Nvidia, Meta, Microsoft and Tesla. Source: Chief Investment Office. As of October 9, 2026.
But recessionary concerns hung like threatening clouds over the markets for most of
2023. Aggressive Fed tightening, an inverted yield curve, and soft economic data were

all taken as harbingers of an economic slowdown and profits recession. Yet the S&P 500
continued to climb in 2023, up 26.3% for the year, driven in large part by the outsized
price performance of the Magnificent Seven.
Investors spent much of 2024 fretting about a market too dependent on technology,
and therefore a market too narrow and too expensive. The unwinding of the Japan carry
trade in August 2024 engendered more angst, as did uncertainties around the 2024

U.S. presidential election. But despite a host of misgivings, the S&P 500 delivered total
returns of 25% in 2024. The unloved bull marched on.
Total returns in 2025 were robust as well—roughly 18%—notwithstanding—yet
again—a number of headwinds. In late January, DeepSeek, a cheaper China AI model,
raised questions about America’s technology leadership and the need to spend enormous
sums on chips and data centers. The Nasdaq promptly dropped by 3.1% thereafter.

Investment Implications
The adoption phase of the AI buildout
should translate to broader earnings
growth and performance across
sectors, reinforcing our conviction
in U.S. Equities. Given a strong
fundamental backdrop, we would
continue to buy on weakness as the
bull enters its fifth year

### Page 5

5 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
January’s jolt was followed by “Liberation Day,” or the warning of punishing U.S. tariffs
on America’s major trading partners. The trade-policy shock of April 2025 caused the
S&P 500 to drop nearly 19% from its February high to its April 8 low on uncertainty
that higher import costs would decrease corporate margins and consumer spending and
upend global supply chains. Amid the trade tumult, Treasury prices fell and the U.S. dollar

weakened on concerns that foreigners were abandoning U.S. assets. Remember all the
angst over the dollar debasement trade? If that wasn’t enough, expensive valuations and
enormous capital commitments raised doubts about the AI trade and triggered talk of an
AI bubble. Yet despite this wall of worry, the S&P 500, for the third year running, posted
double-digit gains in 2025.
And double-digit gains could be in store this year, with the S&P 500 up 14% through

October 9. The bull has rumbled on, taking in stride the energy shock from the war in
the Middle East, the shift in interest rate expectations from the Fed (from rate cuts
to rate hikes), and the rising uncertainty associated with the midterm elections. These
headwinds have been countered by tailwinds such as better-than-expected corporate
earnings and better-than-anticipated real growth.
Finally, as Exhibit 3 makes clear, what has given this bull so much muscle has been

the U.S. Technology sector, specifically the Magnificent 7. Powering the bull has been
the Magnificent 7, with a staggering 344% return since the 2022 lows, while U.S.
technology has delivered a 277% return versus the S&P 500’s return of 130%. Note the
outperformance of the S&P 500 relative to the main international indices.
Looking forward: Is the bull’s greatest strength also its Achilles’ heel? Bull
markets don’t die of natural causes. And they typically don’t expire because of one

particular signal or indicator. Bull markets turn to bear markets when a cluster of signals/
metrics turn south. Think deteriorating and downward earnings revisions, higher interest
rates, widening credit spreads and tighter lending standards, growth disappointments,
stretched valuations and sentiment, deteriorating market breadth, and an external
shock. Any one of these signals could flash red and not trouble the bull.  But when they
converge or feed off each other, that’s trouble.

In addition, since a great deal of the earnings growth, market leadership and capital
expenditures (capex) spending of this bull market have come from the technology sector,
the bull’s greatest strength could become its greatest vulnerability. AI has become an
investment ecosystem unto itself, and hence the fifth year of the bull market will pivot
in part around AI revenue, frontier model adoption rates, infrastructure spending, free
cash flows, depreciation costs, debt issuance, data center utilization, cloud growth, and

competition via cheaper models and computing alternatives. To put it mildly, there is a
great deal at stake when it comes to the bull and AI.
However, this is where we stand today: We believe the bull will continue to rumble
ahead next year, with AI revenues and productivity gains justifying the costs of the AI
infrastructure. Critically, AI is moving from the buildout phase, dominated by chipmakers
and hyperscaler capex, into the adoption phase, where software firms, healthcare,

finance, energy and a myriad of other firms start to see productivity gains and margin
expansion from rising AI adoption rates. We believe this will give better breadth to the
S&P 500; so too will continued profit growth across various U.S. sectors, AI investment
that starts to pay off, and an economy capable of absorbing setbacks or periodic bouts
of uncertainty.
The path forward won’t be linear—and there will be fits and starts, and market
drawdowns. That’s normal for any market. But that aside, the bull will keep rumbling,

roaming and kicking in 2027.

### Page 6

6 of 8 October 13, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
THE TICKER TAPE
What is our view of the macro and market landscape heading
into 2027 and beyond?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investment Office
A supply side-led economic expansion continues, driven by capital investment in the AI
buildout, solid consumer spending and fiscal tailwinds still blowing, albeit with less force.
This creates a backdrop that keeps nominal growth well-above trend and, importantly,

above the interest cost on national debt.
The interest rate backdrop largely remains the same as of today, but, instead of the
sharp increases that we experienced in 2026, the curve should settle down, and yields
should remain relatively range bound. The Fed is likely to tap on the breaks a couple of
more times before reassessing new inflation, growth and employment trends later in
2027.
Concerns surrounding credit risk and "circular" financing are likely to rise the further we

move into the AI buildout, but we do not expect a default cycle to develop. Rather, we
expect the sharp backup in yields to place pressure namely on lower-quality credits in
areas having a difficult time building the cash flow necessary to pay the higher interest
costs.
We expect a broadening out within the market but technology to still maintain its
heavyweight positioning. As we move from training and evolving the large language
models to agentic, and physical AI new leaders are likely to develop. In addition, we

anticipate AI adopters such as biotechnology, transport, logistics, advance manufacturing
and financial companies to enter new growth and productivity phases.
Lastly, overall earnings growth in the U.S. should be low to mid double-digit percent
stages in 2027, which is about half of the growth from 2026 thus far but still around
double from normal levels.
Credit risks developing and higher energy prices for longer are the main risks to our bull
case for Equities extending further into 2027 and beyond.

Index Definitions
ecurities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

University of Michigan’s Consumer Sentiment Index is a monthly survey of consumer confidence levels in the United States conducted by the University of Michigan.
Conference Board’s Index of Consumer Confidence measures what consumers are feeling about their expected financial situation, whether that's optimistic or pessimistic.
Consumer Price Index is a statistical measure that tracks the average change over time in the prices paid by urban consumers for a representative "market basket" of everyday goods and

services.
Nasdaq Composite Index is a stock market measure that tracks the performance of more than 3,000 stocks listed on the Nasdaq exchange.
MSCI Emerging Markets c is a stock market index that tracks the performance of large-cap and mid-cap companies across 24 developing and fast-growing nations.
MSCI All-Country World Index ex USA is a market-capitalization-weighted stock market index that tracks large- and mid-cap equity performance across developed and emerging markets

worldwide, excluding the United States.
MSCI Europe, Australasia, and the Far East (EAFE) Index is a stock market index that measures the performance of large- and mid-cap companies across 21 developed.
Dow Jones Industrial Average Index is a stock market index that tracks 30 major public companies listed on the New York Stock Exchange (NYSE) and Nasdaq.
S&P 500 Equal-Weighted Index is an alternative version of the standard S&P 500 that gives every constituent company an identical weight of 0.2% during each quarterly rebalance, rather

than weighting companies by their market capitalization.
Russell 2000 Index is a stock market index that tracks the performance of approximately 2,000 small-cap (small-capitalization) public companies in the United States.
S&P 500 sub-sectors and industry groups Global Industry Classification Standard (GICS®)/S&P 500 Total Return Index, including Information Technology Total Return (TR) USD;
Consumer Discretionary TR USD; Industrials TR USD; Real Estate TR USD; Communication Services TR USD; Materials  TR USD; Financials TR USD; Consumer Staples TR USD; Utilities TR USD;

Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

## Document 315: China’s October domestic activity data disappoints across the board

Source PDF: all report pdf/China’s October domestic activity data disappoints across the board.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-16
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

China’s retail sales, ﬁxed-asset investment, and industrial
production all came in weaker than expected in October, a
worrying start to the fourth quarter. Signs of further
stabilisation in property prices oﬀer some rays of hope, but the
domestic economy would beneﬁt from more policy support
Lynn Song
Beijing, China
Retail sales slowed by more than expected
in October
China's retail sales slowed to 0.4% year-on-year in October, down
from 1.1% in September, softer than expectations (market: 0.8%,

ING: 0.7%).
Three categories dragged retail sales with double-digit YoY declines
in October. The ﬁrst was gold and jewellery sales (-28.7%), which
plummeted amid falling gold prices. The second was auto sales
(-21.4%), as demand cooled after years of policy support pulled
forward purchases and accelerated China’s shift to electric vehicles.

### Page 2

Finally, building and decoration materials (-11.2%) were under
pressure as continued property market sluggishness limited
renovation demand.
Other categories fared better, but optimism remains limited. Even
categories that had performed strongly earlier in the year, such as
communication appliances (6.5%) and tobacco & alcohol (2.5%),
saw growth slow in October.
Consumption momentum remains tepid heading into the last leg
of 2026. While a more supportive base eﬀect could help the data in

the months ahead, this year will likely mark the worst for
consumption since the pandemic. Moving forward, we will likely
need new methods to support consumption, as the trade-in policy
has turned from tailwind to headwind. Persistently weak consumer
conﬁdence remains the biggest obstacle to a sustained recovery in
consumption, making measures that restore household conﬁdence
essential to any eﬀective policy response. Two main channels to
restore conﬁdence are stabilising asset prices and encouraging

stronger wage growth.
Retail sales growth pulled back in October
Industrial production slows despite solid
external demand
Industrial production slowed to 4.8% YoY in October, down from
5.5% in September, missing expectations (market: 5.3%, ING: 5.5%).
Year-to-date growth remained at 5.3%, keeping industrial

### Page 3

production a relatively bright spot, although October’s data
suggest momentum is softening.
Manufacturing outperformed the headline, growing 5.4% YoY,
compared with 0.7% for mining and 4.4% for utilities. High-tech
manufacturing remained the key source of strength, accelerating to
17.6% YoY. At the industry level, computer, communication and
other electronic equipment manufacturing rose 19.4%, while
special equipment increased 11.5% followed by rail, ships &

aerospace at 9.2%, general equipment at 8.5%, and electrical
machinery at 8.1%.
Solid external demand continued to support industrial activity.
Export delivery value rose 18.6% YoY in nominal terms, remaining
consistent with resilient merchandise exports. Product-level data
showed similar strength in selective high-tech segments.
Semiconductor production rose 21.9% YoY, industrial robots
increased 26.0%, and NEV production grew 17.1%. Yet strength

remained uneven, with microcomputer and smartphone output
recording double-digit declines.
Weakness was also evident in the traditional economy and
domestic demand-linked sectors. Total vehicle production fell
-7.7%, led by a -23.7% decline in passenger cars. Property- and
construction-linked products remained under pressure, with ﬂat
glass down -16.0%, steel products falling -5.1% and cement
declining -1.3%.
Overall, industrial production remained supported by high-tech

manufacturing and exports-related products. However, October’s
slowdown and persistent weakness in domestic-demand sectors
point to the continued unevenness of industrial growth.

### Page 4

Fixed asset investment growth
unexpectedly deteriorated
FAI growth fell sharply to a new low of -8.1% YoY ytd in October,
down from -7.2% YoY ytd. This came in signiﬁcantly worse than
expected (market: -7.0%, ING: -6.7%) and shows investment
remains under pressure despite recent eﬀorts to accelerate ﬁscal
spending and the use of bond proceeds.
Continued weakness on the private side was expected, but the data
came in even weaker than anticipated, with private-led investment

falling to -11.4% YoY ytd, down from -10.4% YoY ytd in the ﬁrst
three quarters of the year. The bigger factor was the public side.
After seeing an uptick in public-led investment in last month's data,
public-led investment fell to -4.0% YoY ytd, down from -3.2% YoY
ytd over the ﬁrst three quarters of the year. This month's data
suggests the September rise was a blip rather than the start of a
recovery.
In terms of the subcategories, there weren't many silver linings. Hi-

tech FAI remained the outperformer (4.0%), but it decelerated for a
second consecutive month. The contraction of real estate (-21.6%),
infrastructure (-5.1%), and manufacturing (-2.9%) FAI worsened
again this month.
Weak investment looks to remain a signiﬁcant overhang on the
economy.

### Page 5

FAI slowed across the board in October
Property market shows further signs of
stabilisation
China's 70-city property price index continued to fall in aggregate,
with new home prices down -0.15% month-on-month and used
home prices down -0.24%, both broadly unchanged from last
month. Property prices have seen 41 consecutive months of
sequential declines, but the monthly drops have slowed this year.
The city-level breakdown continues to oﬀer cause for cautious

optimism amid the data. In October, 21 of 70 cities saw new home
prices stabilise or pick up, while 12 of 70 cities saw used home
prices stabilise or rise, the highest proportion in 5 months. Tier 1
cities continued to show a price recovery, while this has spread to
several tier 2 cities as well. Take Shanghai, for example: secondary
market prices have now picked up for 9 straight months, the
longest streak since the property bust began. After a few fakeouts,

this time the recovery at China's core looks more convincing.
In our 10 questions for China in 2027 report, we discuss whether
the property market's drag is expected to fade. The stabilisation of
prices in tier-one cities is an important ﬁrst step toward restoring
conﬁdence. However, a broader recovery in the property sector will
require not only ﬁrmer prices but also a more convincing reduction
in housing inventories before investment can rebound.

## Document 347: First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-18)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impression_ RBNZ survey of inflation expectations, December quarter 2026 (2026-11-18).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-11-18
Pages: 10
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#inflation #newzealand
First Impression: RBNZ survey of inflation expectations,
December quarter 2026
By Satish Ranchhod
Senior Economist, Westpac NZ
13:50 November 17 2026 Share
Inflation expectations remained contained in the RBNZ’s latest survey. High oil prices and
elevated near-term inflation don’t appear to have boosted medium-term inflation
expectations.
RBNZ inflation expectations survey

### Page 2

1 year ahead: 2.92% (Prev: 2.60%, up 32bps)
2 years ahead: 2.36% (Prev: 2.34%, up 2bps)
5 years ahead: 2.17% (Prev: 2.31%, down 14bps)
10 years ahead: 2.13% (Prev: 2.20%, down 7bp)

There was only limited movement in the RBNZ’s latest survey of inflation expectations. 
Importantly for the RBNZ’s policy deliberations ahead of their December policy meeting, the detail of
today’s update points to inflation expectations remaining well contained despite the current oil-related
lift in inflation.

Looking at the details of the December inflation expectations update:
Expectations for inflation 1 year ahead did rise to 2.92% (vs 2.60% last quarter). That’s consistent with
the sharp rise in fuel prices in recent months, which we expect will push overall inflation up to 4.3% by
the end of this year. 
However, expectations for inflation at longer horizons were much more stable. Notably, the closely
watched measure of inflation in two years’ time nudged just slightly higher, rising to 2.36% (vs 2.34%
previously). 
Similarly, expectations for inflation at long horizons (5 and 10 years ahead) actually slipped back this
quarter and now sit just a little above 2%.

Implications for the RBNZ
Today’s result will be welcome news for the RBNZ. With oil prices pushing inflation to high levels, there
was a risk that expectations for inflation would take a step higher, adding to the pressure on prices over
the year ahead. Such risks had been alluded to in a recent speech by the RBNZ’s Chief Economist.
Nevertheless, the RBNZ is still looking at an elevated near-term inflation outlook. Importantly, the
strength in inflation isn’t just due to high oil prices. Core inflation has remained at firm levels for an
extended period. 
Consistent with those firm underlying inflation pressures, we continue to expect an OCR hike at the
RBNZ’s upcoming 9 December policy meeting. 
We’ll discuss our thoughts in more detail in our latest Economic Overview, out tomorrow.

Satish Ranchhod, Senior Economist

Standard Chartered Bank

Charts of the week: Watching earnings and a potential ‘Blue wave’

US Big Tech earnings, mid-term elections and major central bank policy meetings are the near-term market drivers 
Consensus S&P500 index Q3 earnings estimates by sector   Chance of Democrats winning control of US House and Senate*

Source: Bloomberg, Standard Chartered; *Polymarket probability

Editorial 
Policy, politics and earnings  
Strategy summary: We retain our near-term defensive and 
diversified stance across assets, sectors and regions amid 
near-term uncertainty around monetary policy, politics and 
earnings. The next two weeks will be busy for major central 
banks, US political watchers and technology leaders powering 
the AI supercycle. We expect the Fed, ECB and BoJ to hold 
rates next week, but markets will focus on whether central 
banks are turning more hawkish due to the latest surge in fuel 
prices. The US mid-term elections are expected to give 
Democrats control of the House and Senate, with implications 
for taxes and the AI-led investment boom.  
Meanwhile, the consensus is expecting the US tech sector to 
report over 60% y/y earnings growth for Q3, the biggest jump 
in over two decades outside the post-COVID rebound.  Each of 
these three drivers have the potential to surprise markets.  
Big Three to hold monetary policy: We expect the Fed, ECB 
and BoJ to hold policy rates next week, after September’s 
hikes, as they assess whether higher bond yields have 
tightened financial conditions enough to offset fuel-price-driven 
inflation. We expect the Big Three to hike next in December as 
fuel prices are likely to stay elevated into early next year due to 
refining capacity constraints. Nevertheless, the Fed and ECB 
are likely to deliver less than around 60bps of rate hikes priced 
in by money markets over the next 12 months on the back of 
an eventual easing of oil and fuel prices leading to disinflation 
by H2 2027. This outlook means 3-7-year Developed Market 
investment grade bonds currently offer attractive risk-reward.  
Opportunity in Australian bonds. Other major central banks, 
such as the Bank of England, Bank of Canada and Reserve 
Bank of New Zealand are likely to hike rates by 25bps in the 
next two weeks given their focus on taming near-term inflation. 
However, we believe the Reserve Bank of Australia (RBA) is 
close to the end of its hiking cycle, even if it hikes one last time 
in the coming months (money markets give a 60% chance of a 
hike on 3 November). Australia’s domestic labour and housing 
markets and business confidence are softening after the RBA 
hiked its policy rate four times this year to a 15-year high. 
Meanwhile, Australia’s cash rate is 60bps higher than the US 
policy rate, supporting the AUD. We continue to see attractive 
income and FX gain opportunities from AUD corporate bonds.  
A ‘Blue Wave’ in the US? Prediction markets strongly favour 
Democrats taking the House of Representatives and possibly 
the Senate in the 3 November US mid-term elections. While an 
opposition sweep in the mid-terms has historically delivered the 
best 12-month returns for equities among all scenarios, partly 
due to the resulting policy gridlock, a decisive Democratic “blue 
wave” could increase longer-term risks of higher taxes and 
uncertainty over policy towards AI investment and data centres. 
With the Senate race still close and prediction markets fallible, 
we remain cautious on risk assets in the near term.  
Closing EU bank contingent convertible bonds (CoCos1) 
and US 10-year government bond ideas: We lock in 2% 
gains in EU CoCos and 1.7% gain in US 10-year government 
bond ideas amid French and US political risks (page 5-6). We 
continue to hedge any inflation risks through inflation-protected 
bonds and geopolitical risk through an Overweight in gold.  
Watching Big Tech earnings:  Upcoming Big Tech earnings 
raises uncertainty as market leadership remains concentrated 
and share-price reactions more mixed. With valuations less 
likely to drive returns, earnings delivery and sustainability of AI 
investment, funding and monetisation and broadening out of 
earnings beyond tech will be critical, raising the risk of volatility 
and sector divergence. We remain invested but defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification across sectors and regions.  
Staying defensive and diversified. We recently upgraded the 
defensive US Healthcare sector to Overweight, while retaining 
our Overweights to US tech and materials as well as financials 
in the US, Euro area and Japan. We also downgraded the more 
volatile Communications Services sector to Neutral and took 
profits on the semiconductor segment earlier this month before 
the latest correction. Our defensive and diversified stance 
should preserve participation in further equity upside, while 
reducing exposure to earnings disappointments and supporting 
resilience through the reporting season.  
—  Rajat Bhattacharya

### Page 4

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions 
  What are the key takeaways from the technology sector 
earnings reported so far this week? 
Our view: Recent technology sector earnings results point to 
strong compute demand and continued capex expansion, 
supporting our positive long-term view on the AI investment 
theme. However, rising funding and execution risks warrant 
near-term caution. We prefer to avoid increasing exposure to 
higher-risk areas within the technology sector for now. 
Rationale: Earnings results reported this week suggest major tech 
companies remain committed to expanding their AI infrastructure. 
While this bodes well for AI infrastructure providers, ambitious 
expansion plans imply that an increasing share of AI capex is likely 
to depend on debt financing. We expect debt-financed AI capex to 
rise from c.25% of AI capex in 2026 to c.40% in 2027.  
Greater reliance on borrowing increases sensitivity to interest rates, 
while deployment delays could postpone revenue recognition amid 
ongoing financing costs. However, evidence of improving AI 
monetisation and adoption should strengthen operating cash 
flows and support internal capex funding. Against this backdrop, 
we will assess Big Tech earnings next week for greater clarity on 
capex guidance and funding and AI monetisation trends.  
—  Ryan Goh, Investment Strategist 
  Are you still positive on Euro area bank equities amid 
heightened political and fiscal uncertainty in the region? 
Our view: We remain positive on Euro area bank equities, 
supported by resilient earnings and shareholder-friendly capital 
return policies. While France’s fiscal uncertainty and a softer 
growth outlook warrant caution, the recent pullback in Euro 
area bank equities offers selective opportunities. We favour 
well-capitalised banks with strong deposit franchises and 
diversified earnings. 
Rationale: We expect Euro area banks’ Q3 2026 net interest 
income to grow 1.5-3.0% q/q, led by Spanish banks, driven by 
higher rates and continued asset repricing. Resilient fee income from 
wealth management and investment banking activity, as well as 
dividends and buybacks, should provide further support. Price-to-
book (P/B) valuations have eased, despite expectations for 
healthy return on equity (ROE), thus improving risk-reward. 
Fiscal uncertainty in France has widened sovereign spreads, 
pressuring banks’ bond holdings and funding costs. Although 
French banks represent 13% of the Euro area Bank Index, volatility 
could spread to regional financial markets. However, stress in the 
broader banking system appears limited, supported by strong 
capital buffers and ECB tools to contain disorderly market moves and 
sovereign market contagion. We will continue to monitor credit 
trends, sovereign spreads and banks’ 2027 earnings guidance. 
—  Robin Xie, Senior Investment Strategist

### Page 5

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What has driven the recent weakness in China equities, 
and what is your outlook from here? 
Our view: We maintain China equities as a Core holding, as 
valuations remain inexpensive. Rapid technological innovation 
and investment in AI localisation provide tailwinds, but the 
potential for a re-rating appears delayed. Investors should seek 
to diversify their portfolios and reduce concentration risk. 
Rationale: China equities have weakened, driven by mounting 
scepticism over AI capex returns, structural margin 
compression and concerns about the country’s significant 
financing requirements. Another notable feature in China has been 
significant share issuance, which has diluted earnings per share and 
weighed on the MSCI China Index’s performance. These pressures 
have been compounded by geopolitical headwinds, restrictions on 
advanced chips, an enduring property sector contraction and weak 
domestic demand. September’s stimulus measures also 
disappointed markets in terms of their scale and pace of delivery. 
Notwithstanding these headwinds, valuations remain inexpensive, 
supported by rapid domestic innovation and structural tailwinds 
in AI localisation, leading us to retain China equities as a Core 
holding. Elevated volatility reinforces the need to be defensively 
positioned, favouring diversification rather than relying on technology 
sector concentration. We also remain Overweight China’s 
healthcare, financial and materials sectors. 
—  Cindy Lam, CFA, Senior Investment Strategist

Are you still bullish on US 10-year government bonds?   
Our view: We are closing our opportunistic bullish idea on US 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. The risk-reward 
has become less compelling after the yield broke below 5%, the 
lower bound of our three-month forecast range of 5.00-5.25%. 
Rationale: The US 10-year yield fell over 17bps to 4.97% on 22 
October – its first move below 5% since 23 September and biggest 
one-day drop since March 2023. A sell-off in AI-related equities and 
renewed Middle East tensions drove investors towards safe havens. 
Earlier this month, the yield hit its highest level in over two decades. 
The forces behind the rally are fragile, though. Oil prices remain 
elevated amid escalating Middle East tensions, and September US 
consumer inflation rose to 3.7% y/y. Fed officials have signalled 
there is no urgency to raise rates at the 27-28 October meeting, 
but persistent price pressures keep further tightening on the table. 
We prefer to lock in gains and close our opportunistic bullish idea on 
10-year government bonds at a gain of 1.7%. 
—  Jonathan Liang, CFA, CIO, Fixed Income & FX

Significant share issuance has kept the market 
capitalisation fairly stable, while the MSCI China 
Index has declined c.20% over the past one year

### Page 6

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  What is your outlook for French bank bonds, especially 
ahead of France’s upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews?   
Our view: The recent sell-off in French bank bonds is a repricing 
of tail risks, not a credit story. We expect further price volatility 
until the April 2027 French presidential election, particularly at 
the junior end of the capital structure, so we are closing our 
opportunistic EU bank contingent convertibles (CoCos1) idea at 
a gain of 2.0%. 
Rationale: We believe the recent sell-off in French bank bonds 
reflects domestic political and fiscal risks rather than weaker 
credit fundamentals. The sustained widening of the OAT-Bund 
spread has raised concerns about the profit and loss (P&L) impact 
on banks' sovereign debt holdings. Banks provide limited 
disclosure on these exposures. However, banks’ investment 
portfolios are generally diversified, and holdings booked at amortised 
cost should soften the P&L impact. Near-term catalysts include the 
upcoming sovereign credit rating reviews. A sovereign downgrade 
could trigger bank rating downgrades, with domestically focused 
lenders potentially more exposed than regionally diversified peers. A 
sovereign credit event is not our base case, but we expect headline 
risks to persist until the French presidential election. Against this 
backdrop, we are closing our opportunistic EU bank CoCos idea. 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist 
  Will the November US mid-term elections and European 
bond volatility support a sustained Swiss franc (CHF) rebound?  
Our view: We expect a near-term CHF rebound, supported 
mainly by safe-haven demand amid European bond volatility. 
We believe the US mid-term elections will be a secondary driver. 
Rationale: European sovereign bond volatility provides the stronger 
catalyst for CHF strength. The France-Germany government bond 
yield spread remains elevated near 160bps, reflecting heightened 
French fiscal concerns and reinforcing the CHF’s safe-haven appeal. 
The US mid-terms could provide additional support for the CHF. 
A Democratic victory in either chamber is expected to constrain 
further fiscal expansion, potentially weighing on the USD. However, 
this would not necessarily lead to faster fiscal consolidation. 
The SNB’s 0% policy rate leaves the CHF at a sizeable interest-
rate disadvantage vs. the USD, constraining sustained CHF gains. 
With EUR/CHF below 0.90, excessive CHF strength could also 
increase the risk of SNB intervention. A decisive USD/CHF break 
below 0.8130 would strengthen the technical case for a more 
sustained CHF rebound.  
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Iris Yuen, Investment Strategist

A widening France-Germany 10-year government 
bond yield spread and a stable investment-grade 
(IG) bond option-adjusted spread (OAS) suggest a 
repricing of political and fiscal risk rather than 
credit risk

### Page 7

Standard Chartered Bank 
WS Global CIO Office | 23 October 2026

Top client questions (cont’d) 
  Would worsening budget deficits and political uncertainty 
across parts of Europe affect your view on EUR/USD and Euro 
area government bond yields? 
Our view: Fiscal and political concerns constrain near-term 
EUR/USD gains, with France being the primary vulnerability. We 
expect EUR/USD to recover towards the 1.17-1.18 range over 
the next 12 months, supported by continued Euro area 
economic growth and an expected fading of USD support. We 
expect wider differentials in Euro area government bond yields, 
with political risk premium elevated in French government 
bonds (OATs) and German government bonds (Bunds) 
supported by safe-haven demand. 
Rationale: France's high public debt, large budget deficit and 
political obstacles to fiscal adjustments are weighing on market 
confidence, with the country’s 10-year government bond yield 
rising above 5%. The risk of stress spreading to Italy and Spain 
adds to regional concerns, although current evidence does not 
establish a broad European fiscal crisis. German government bonds 
should benefit from safe-haven demand, while fiscal concerns keep 
risk premia on vulnerable countries' bonds elevated. EUR/USD 
remains more sensitive to US-Germany interest-rate differentials 
and broader market confidence than to French bond yields alone. 
France’s fiscal risks, elevated energy costs and high US interest 
rates weigh on the EUR in the near term. Over the next 12 months, 
Germany’s multi-year public investment programme, resilient 
exports and sound banking-sector fundamentals are expected to 
support Euro area growth. We expect the Fed's rate-hiking cycle to 
peak by mid-2027, reducing USD support and driving the EUR  
towards 1.17-1.18 . 
The principal tail risk is fiscal stress widening government bond 
yield spreads and spilling over into bank funding conditions and 
lending activity, weakening growth and delaying the EUR's recovery. 
—  Vincent Tan, Senior Investment Strategist 
—  Cedric Lam, Senior Investment Strategist

France’s fiscal risks constrain near-term EUR/USD 
gains, but a modest recovery remains likely

## Document 409: Rates Spark_ Can AI confidence bring rates back up_

Source PDF: all report pdf/Rates Spark_ Can AI confidence bring rates back up_.pdf
Report source: ING Think
Date: 2026-11-17
Pages: 3
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Nvidia will announce its quarterly earnings today, followed a
day later by Anthropic's IPO, two major AI events. Macro has
become closely intertwined with AI sentiment and therefore
positive and negative surprises should not be ignored by the
bond market. Having said that, we think fading spillovers to
euro rates will often make sense
Michiel Tukker
We’ve just come through a period of fragile AI sentiment, which has had a
direct impact on rates
Corporate earnings announcements now

part of the macro realm
We’ll be keeping an eye on Nvidia’s earnings announcement as
these results have become an important bellwether for the broader
AI story. The stakes are high, with Nvidia making up 5% of the S&P
500 market capitalisation. We’ve just come through a period of
fragile AI sentiment, which has had a direct impact on rates.

### Page 2

The 10yr UST yield is still down from the previous highs in October
on the back of AI jitters. But with the NASDAQ up again, we think
both US and EUR rates can continue heading back to their previous
peaks. If Nvidia earnings don’t disappoint and Anthropic manages
to pull oﬀ a successful IPO, bonds are unlikely to come out as a
winner.
On the other hand, failing to meet high AI expectations can trigger
a signiﬁcant move lower in rates too. AI-driven investments have

become an integral part of the US growth story. Markets
contemplating a 50bp hike in December goes hand in hand with
conﬁdence that AI will continue to support the economy. Any signs
of setbacks could make markets more cautious and tilt
expectations in a more dovish direction.
An AI-driven risk-oﬀ event will likely also pull down euro rates, but
we would suggest fading such moves, especially at the front end.
First, the ECB’s reaction function is more driven by inﬂation than

growth at the moment. Second, AI doesn’t have a big footprint (yet)
in eurozone growth. An exception would be if worsening risk
sentiment also hurts French government bonds. In that case, a
more dovish market position could be warranted.
Tuesday’s events and market views
After employment data from the UK, we'll turn to the ZEW
survey outcomes for Germany and the eurozone. Consensus
doesn't expect much change, but, if anything, maintains a
slightly positive bias. From the US, we have retail sales,

import prices, industrial production and capacity utilisation
numbers for October. In particular, retail data will be watched
for signs of price developments and the strength of US
consumers.
The UK will auction £3.25bn of 14y Gilts. Germany will
auction €5bn of 2y Schatz. Finland will auction 4y and 10y
RFGBs for a total of €1.50bn.

Time to ﬁnd 2027 US listed
soaring stocks
上週精選了 20 家於 2027-2028 營收及獲利增⻑將明顯優於同業的台
灣科技及半導體公司，這週精選了美國 20 家，但加⼊了 adj EPS
yoy ，⽑利及營業利潤率， rule of 40 ，及 PEG 估值⽐較
陸⾏之  ANDREW LU ON SEMI/TECHS

READ IN APP
Executive summary
上週我們提到接近年末，市場都會棄舊迎新尋找明年飆股及本夢⽐⾏情，主要

是針對營收及獲利仍有⼤幅增⻑潛⼒的公司，都會在獲利還沒爆發前提前先
跑，⽽造成  P/E 估值升級，尤其投資⼈在經歷 10-11 ⽉份的全球科技及半導體
產業股價⼤修正超過 30% 之後 ( 費半從歷史⾼點 14,655 回檔達 35% ），我們精
選了 20 家於 2027-2028 營收及獲利增⻑將明顯優於同業的台灣科技及半導體
公司（請參考之前報告：Time to ﬁnd 2027 soaring stocks），這次我們來
精選美國 20 家營收及獲利快速增⻑的科技及半導體公司，但加⼊了 adj EPS
yoy, 並⽤⽑利及營業利潤率變化來判斷營運槓桿差異，⽐較 rule of 40 預測
及⽐較未來 4 年 PEG 估值是否合理？

### Page 2

Source: Bloomberg
Top 20 US listed growing names in the sectors
因考慮量⼦電腦，⼩型核電站 SMR ，及如⾬後春筍冒出來的⼩型新興雲（⾃
Jensen 從 Nvidia 離職後，我們相信  Nvidia 對於⼩型新興雲的財務挹注會⼤
幅降低）的產業及公司獲利模式⾄今仍存有極⼤不確定性及⾵險，所以在這次
的篩選中，我們決定暫時將這三個族群的產業及相關的公司排除⽐較，但仍會
持續關注這些產業的變化。在篩選及排序彭博社分析師超過 100 家在美國市場
交易的主要科技及半導體公司  2027/2028 年度及季度營收 / 獲利預測後，我
們精選出前 20 名公司 2027 年營收及 Adj EPS 增⻑最快，獲利有機會倍增的公
司，從這些公司的⽐較表中，我們可以發現哪些公司有較⾼的營運槓桿⽑利率
及營業利潤率變化 ? 哪些公司有較⾼的 rule of 40? 哪些公司的 adj EPS P/E,
PEG 估值偏⾼？這其中包括 5 家 AI 數據算⼒中⼼及系統組裝公司， 7 家 AI 運算及

AI ⾼速互聯晶⽚公司， 3 家光纖通訊雷射光相關公司， 2 家晶⽚測試設備公司，
剩下 3 家分佈在 AI 解決⽅案，天然氣發電機，及燃料電池產業。
付費訂閱戶可分享我們精選的 20 家美國上市公司年度 /
季度營收年增排名及哪些公司的  adj EPS 轉虧為盈，
從⽑利及營業利潤率排序找哪些公司有⾼營運槓桿，⽐
較  superior rule of 40 ，哪些公司修正整理後估值仍

偏⾼？及分享 4 ⼤產業⾵險，參與投票，並可索取報告

Names with stronger sales growth
新興雲短期營收倍增可期，但⻑期成⻑⾵險待察：從 2027 年度及季度營收年
增來分析，營收年增倍增的公司主要集中在  新興 AI 數據算⼒中⼼  Neocloud
如 SpaceXSI, Nebius, 及 CoreWeave ，雖然這些公司的主要晶⽚供應商
Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang 最近離職⽽群⿓無⾸，循環融資及未來新產品技
術是否如期推出不明朗，外加這些新興雲公司要持續擴充  Nvidia GPU 機櫃算
⼒來完成營收倍增的⻑期資本開⽀有短缺⾵險，但我們認為其 3-4 年的積壓訂
單（請參考下⾯圖表）及使⽤客戶預付款來補⾜資本開⽀現⾦流出及考量其他
⼩型新興雲將提早被淘汰出局，我們認為 SpaceXSI, Nebius, 及 CoreWeave
這三家新興雲要完成 2027 年營收倍增，應該不難。
Source: The companies, Andrew Lu on global semis/techs

新興 AI 推理晶⽚，測試設備，光纖雷射營收倍增受惠：跟 AMD 在 AI 推理⽅⾯
密切合作的  Cerebras System 系統晶⽚公司，也是採⽤台積電  System on
wafer SoW 製程技術的主要客戶。 Aehr Test Systems 主要受惠於  AI
GPU/ASIC 晶⽚的晶圓級預燒與測試  (Wafer-Level Test & Burn-In, WLTBI)
設備需求。 Applied Optoelectronics 及 AXT 受惠於 AI 數據算⼒中⼼的光纖
互聯雷射光需求的爆發。 Nvidia 因 CEO Jensen Huang 離職，雖然短期 Vera

### Page 4

Rubin 需求仍⼤幅優於供給不受影響，但  AMD AI 加速器 / 機櫃及⾕歌，亞⾺
遜， Meta ， OpenAI ⾃研晶⽚競爭，主權 AI 市場（雖說主權，但很多轉租算⼒
是給中國⼤陸客戶）陸續被美國商務部要求管制，彭博社分析師仍預測 Nvidia
營收年增於 2H28 明顯減速（ vs. 75% yoy in 2027) 。
2028 營收增⻑減速？雖然彭博社分析師普遍預期美國精選 20 其 2028 年營收
增⻑會明顯減速，但我們也不排除是分析師採保守預測，我們會持續關注 AI 訂
閱及 Tokens 需求的變化， AI 代理⼈及實體世界模型  World model 應⽤的進
展，數據中⼼電⼒及資本開⽀資⾦的取得是否順利。即使 2028 年的營收增⻑
的變數增⼤，但分析師還是預期  Nebius 及  Cerebras 的營收倍增。
Source: Bloomberg, Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Adj EPS turning into positive from earlier losses

不同於台灣投資⼈沒有  non-GAAP 或調整後財報數字作為參考，美國會計準
則及分析師通常將併購後的 5-6 年商譽攤銷，⾮經常性費⽤排除在調整後⽑利
率，營業利潤率，及 EPS 之外，然後來分析其獲利變化。歸因於新興雲公司，
SoW AI 公司  Cerebras, 新興測試設備公司  Aehr 及 Cohu ，光纖通訊 InP 基
板公司  AXT 於 2026 年上半年仍多處於虧損狀態， Adj EPS 年增⽐較數字就變
得沒有意義，所以我們的 adj EPS 排序是⽐較 2028 年  adj EPS 年增的幅度，
前 5 名明顯跟 AI 運算及光纖互聯晶⽚相關。
但就從投資獲利及⾵險兩⽅來衡量，我們還是建議投資⼈以規模較⼤的⾼獲利
增⻑⿓頭公司作為核⼼持股（ SpaceXSI, Marvell, Broadcom, AMD) ，但配
合其他⾼獲利增⻑新趨勢公司作為衛星組合。

### Page 5

Source: Bloomberg, Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Emerging players have more operating leverage
從  GAAP ⽑利率及營業利潤率⽐較來看， 2027 到 2028 明顯有⾼營運槓桿三
率三升（營收增⻑，⽑利率提升，營業利潤率提升）的公司主要為台積電的
SoW AI 晶⽚由虧轉盈的主⼒客戶  Cerebras 及提供光纖通訊 EML 雷射晶⽚的
Applied Optoelectronics 。但新興雲公司如  Nebius 及 SpaceXSI 即使⽑利
率變化不⼤，靠著擴⼤營運規模且控制研發，銷售，管理費⽤，營業利潤率從
2027 到 2028 也有雙位數的增幅。這可以解釋這幾家公司為何其  adj EPS 增
幅也是名列前茅。⽽⽐較沒有營運槓桿的公司主要有⽑利率及營業利潤率已在
歷史⾼點的公司如 Nvidia ，博通， Astera Labs ，及 Credo 。
Source: Bloomberg, Andrew Lu on global semis/techs

Superior rule of 40

### Page 6

歸因於我們精選的 20 家美國上市的科技及半導體公司都是營收⾼增⻑公司，加
上⼤多數公司都有雙位數的營業利潤率（除了⾕歌主要的系統組裝公司
Celestica 只有 7% 營業利潤率外），所以其  rule of 40 指標都遠遠⾼於產業
平均， 2027 年超過 100% 的有 12 家，即使 2028 年營收年增減速，超過 100%
的仍有 5 家。
Source: Andrew Lu on global semis/techs
Valuation matters
美國精選 20 中有 7 家（ Cerebras System, Applied Optoelectronics,
SpaceXSI, Cohu, AXT, FTAI Aviation, AMD) 有機會在 2027 年的  adj EPS
倍增，⽽前 3 家  adj EPS 2026-2030 年複合增⻑率皆超過 100% （ Applied
Optoelectronics adj EPS 預測僅到 2028 ），尤其在 10-11 ⽉份科技及半導體

公司股價⼤幅拉回之後，美國精選 20 的⼤部分公司在短期來看都已回到低於
30x 2027 adj EPS 的  P/E 估值 , 雖然  Cerebras ， SpaceXSI ， Palantir ，
Bloom Energy ，  Aehr 都還交易在 30x 以上的  2027 adj EPS ，但如果⽤
2027-2030 年 4 年平均  P/E 來跟 2026-2030 年 4 年  adj EPS 複合增⻑率來⽐
較，這些公司的 4 年平均  PEG 都還是維持在 1.0 以下，這其中較為偏⾼的也只
有  Palantir 的 0.82 PEG 及 Aehr 的 0.99 PEG ⽐較接近 1 。
我們從最近美國精選 20 的股價震盪回檔來看，雖然短中期基本⾯仍然紮實，
但⻑期投資⼈已經開始考量以下 4 ⼤⾵險是否將影響⻑期營收 /adj EPS 獲利
預測及 PEG 估值被下修的⾵險。  1. Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang 離職對
Nvidia 及 AI 產業技術疊代群⿓無⾸的⻑期影響； 2. 美國⺠主黨參眾議院及州

⻑選舉⼤勝是否將嚴重放緩 AI 基礎建設，還是也想分⼀杯羹； 3. AI 數據算⼒
中⼼的基礎建設是否將被缺電，缺電網，缺資⾦⽽減緩？ 4. 很多轉租算⼒給
中國⼤陸客戶的主權 AI 市場是否被美國商務部擴⼤管制？

### Page 7

考，並⾮投資建議，個⼈應審慎評估⾵險，如因此造成任何損失，本⼈不負任

熱門議題 / 投資關鍵
《AI晶片與相關硬體獲利持續創新高》
根據機構LSEG統計，受到AI晶片、記憶體、硬碟等
科技公司獲利優於預期，機構上調科技產業4Q26、
1Q27獲利年增率到56.2%、54.6%，本行樂觀看待
科技產業發展後勢。10月份重要科技硬體晶圓代工
龍頭台積電3Q26法說表示，受益於強勁AI需求與2
奈米開始貢獻營收，公司3Q26營收與獲利均優於市
場預期。3Q26高效運算與智慧手機營收比重分別達
68%、21%。高效運算營收年增高達53%、智慧手
機年減10%。AI代理與開源模型普及帶動先進製程

用量， 台 積 電 預 估 4Q26 美 元 營 收 將 年 增 62% ，
2026年美元營收預估達44%。由於AI晶片成長高於
60%，預估台積電2027年營收與獲利仍將續創新高。

2
重點摘要
• 雲端巨頭資本支出持平，伺
服器仍優於消費終端
《雲端巨頭資本支出持平，伺服器仍優於消費終端》
3Q26財報顯示，谷哥、亞馬遜、微軟與Meta在
2026年資本支出目標合計達7,300億美元，較上季
略減25億美元(-0.3%)，減少主要是微軟外部第三方
算力產能延遲導致資本支出目標下修50億美元，與
Meta上調25億美元相互抵消。雖然雲端巨頭支出
持平但資料中心晶片需求仍強勁。英特爾3Q26財報
顯示，受益於資料中心與AI事業帶動營收達165億
美元、年增21%。其中資料中心與人工智慧相關營
收73億美元續創歷史新高、年增78%，2027年相關
晶片仍將供不應求。反觀個人電腦營收81億美元、
年減5%。英特爾預估2026年個人電腦出貨量將年

減12%、2027年受到記憶體與儲存供給影響預估將
續減10%。
受到上半年消費者預期終端售價上漲提前換機，
3Q26個人電腦與智慧手機出貨均呈現大幅下滑。根
據IDC統計，3Q26全球個人電腦出貨量達6,270萬
台，較2025年同期衰退20%。同樣受到記憶體漲價
與供應短缺衝擊的智慧手機3Q26出貨量2.6億台，
較2025年同期衰退22%。由於未來記憶體與儲存價
格小幅調漲以及供給受限下，消費終端價格仍將維
持高價位。因此，IDC預估個人電腦與智慧手機
2027年將高機率維持衰退，預估要2028年出貨量
才有望復甦。
資料來源：公司，2026/10/30，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/30

重點摘要
3
《類比晶片終端需求上升，工業、資料中心占比較
高供應商營收更具成長性》
德州儀器(TXN)3Q26營收60.6億美元、EPS 2.62美
元雙雙優於預期，4Q26財測營收58-63億美元，中
位數較市場普遍預期高出6%，反映資料中心市場
需求持續成長，使營收預測優於往年季節性回落。
以3Q26終端市場來看，工業營收年增超過40%、
季增約10%，成長來自航太國防、能源基礎設施等
領域；車用營收年增約15%、季增約10%，受益於
客戶庫存水準降低及電動車占比提高；資料中心營
收則年增約95%、季增20%，為成長最快的終端市
場。在營運方面，產能利用率提高以及2027年潛在
的價格調漲可能進一步擴大獲利成長空間，儘管資

本支出可能高於2026年的20~30億美元，但顯示終
端需求正擴大成長。
恩智浦(NXPI)3Q26營收37.9億美元、EPS 4.20美元
優於預期，但4Q26財測營收中位數38.5億美元略
低於市場預期。以3Q26終端市場來看，車用營收
20.7億美元、年增13%，成長主要來自軟體定義汽
車(SDV)產品貢獻；工業與物聯網營收8.02億美元、
年增39%，主要由資料中心、工廠自動化和醫療保
健等產業帶動；通訊基礎設施營收5.03億美元、年
增54%，得益於資料中心業務成長。恩智浦財報結
果顯示各終端市場需求持續上升，惟成長速度較慢
的車用營收占比超過五成，未來成長速度可能不及
工業和資料中心營收占比更高的同業。
• 德州儀器4Q26 財 測 營 收 較
市場預期高出6%，反映資料
中心市場需求大幅成長。
• 德儀產能利用率提高及2027
年潛在的價格調漲可能進一
步擴大獲利成長空間。

• 券商 CIO 調查顯示 IT 採購預
算與軟體支出預期持續增溫。
• AI/ML 與網安軟體優先性持續
領先，但 ERP、BI、CRM 等
應用軟體優先性也見改善。
4
《券商 CIO 調查顯示軟體支出預期持續增溫》
券商 CIO 調查指出 2027 年的企業 IT 採購預算仍維
持成長。CIO 現在預計 2026 年的 IT 採購預算將年
增 4.0%，自 2Q26 走高 20bps，連續 3 次增溫。
對於 2027 年 IT 採購預算的首次估計，CIO 預期年
增 4.0%，雖僅與更新後的 2026 年預期相符，但實
則是近 4 年來的開年新高。在這之中，軟體預算成
長優於領先硬體、網通與服務，預期年增 4.4%，且

自 2026 年加速 10bps。2027 年軟體支出的預期加
速可能進一步說明短期內 AI 並未帶來全面性衝擊。
資料來源：摩根史丹利，2026/10/12，中國信託商業銀行整理，2026/10/20
人工智慧與機器學習 (AI/ML) 的支出優先性仍穩居
CIO 調查的第一名，佔比雖較前幾期調研回落，但
這應該是因為 CIO 對於開始增加其他項目的優先性。
AI 相關專案的預算仍最不可能被刪除，且淨占比
(即最不可能被刪除 – 最可能被刪除的比例)，3Q26
較 2Q26 大幅走揚 13ppts 至 17%。網路安全的支
出優先性則連 4 季走揚，且最不可能被刪除的淨占
比也走揚 1ppt 至 16%。持續反映出 AI 帶動的攻擊
面擴大、攻擊手法更加多元，與攻擊成本大幅走低，
導致企業在 AI 顯著產生正面效益前，優先選擇防範
未然的心態。而隨著 AI 的能力仍在快速發展，如近
期 Anthropic 研究即指出 AI 已可自我迭代，且小
型模型未來可能具備在物聯網裝置上自我複製的能

力，本行預期網安的需求可望仍持續強健。數位轉
型雖仍處第 3，但在本次券商調查中大幅地低於過
硬體 軟體 通訊設備 IT服務
■3Q25 ■4Q25 ■1Q26 ■2Q26 ■3Q26
2026 外部 IT 支出期望

人可更加關注各應用軟體在此領域推進的表現。
